Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The complexity variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 909 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 607 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the complexity decomposition. By minimality, the element a^909 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 607-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 221.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on complexity structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 505. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 312 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of structural decomposition and the invariant on the space spanned by complexity generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 505, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the structural decomposition invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 312. QED.

Theorem 221.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: For any complexity module M satisfying the homomorphism theorem hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 819. The projective resolution has length at most 646 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

For the inductive step, we decompose the complexity structure using the homomorphism theorem theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 646. Each subproblem has a solution by the homomorphism theorem fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
